24:["$","p",null,{"children":["A quantum processor’s properties can drift faster than the interval between reported QPU updates. One common cause is ",["$","$L1b",null,{"href":"https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-022-00643-y","children":"two-level-systems (TLS) interactions"}],", which fluctuate on short timescales and can temporarily degrade qubit performance. 2c:["$","p",null,{"children":["There are some options we can define when choosing a backend, such as enabling fractional gates. Fractional gates are parameterized quantum gates that enable direct execution of arbitrary-angle rotations (within specific bounds), eliminating the need to decompose them into multiple basis gates. We are using them in this tutorial because they can significantly reduce both the depth and duration of quantum circuits, which helps with the total QPU time of the characetrization experiments described below, but note that they are not required to run characterization experiments in general. 3e:["$","p",null,{"children":"Looking at the reported QPU properties over time (we'll consider a single week below), we see how these can fluctuate on a scale of a single day. Small fluctuations can happen even in shorter timescales, mostly due to unexpected environment interactions. In this scenario, the reported properties will not accurately capture the current status of the QPU. Moreover, if a job is transpiled locally (using current reported properties) and submitted but executed only at a later time (minutes or days), it may run the risk of having used outdated properties for qubit selection in the transpilation step. This highlights the importance of having updated information about the QPU at execution time.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

45:["$","p",null,{"children":"Characterization experiments should be run before transpilation. Once the characterization experiments are completed, their results can be used to update the QPU error properties. These updates directly affect the transpilation step as they provide real-time data on hardware perfromamce. In practice, it is best to run all jobs as close in time as possible, which can be achieved by executing them within a single session.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

aa:["$","p",null,{"children":"Another motivation for running characterization experiments before executing a circuit is to overwrite unrealistic error values. In Qiskit, some gate errors may be reported as 1 when randomized benchmarking fits fail. Although these values do not reflect the true performance of the gate, they can skew predictions.
